### E117 preregistration · 21 November 2026 · Can newborns inherit faster shelter finding?

The owner approved a cold donor versus fresh-start comparison for all three original evolved A genomes. Real-world outcome data for this protocol have not been opened. The hypothesis is that sharing one donor cohort's acquired sensory-to-motor weight changes lets outside-born newborns reach shelter sooner on an unseen world, without a measurable survival loss. All three independent evolution seeds must meet both thresholds below. The thresholds apply separately to each seed.

For each genome, draw 256 independent donor–recipient world families with four assigned creatures per cohort. The two lives have independently drawn worlds and development keys. In each family the donor and the recipients face the same stationary food rule, alternating by family index to give 128 families per rule. Both lives use the default 300-turn grid, with shelter observations, a movement energy cost of 0.01 and an outdoor cold cost of 0.02 starting at turn 151. Use the existing plasticity rules. Keep the optional persistence trait off in both arms. Hold the original genome, newborn wiring, starting positions, world, and action random stream fixed across arms. Reset bodily and neural activity at birth. In one branch initialize the newborns' existing connections with the donor archive. In the other use fresh starting weights. Include all donors, including their last living weights if they die. Clear the archive between families. Do not train or select genomes.

For recipients born outside shelter, record the first turn (1–300) ending alive inside. Assign 301 to every non-arrival, including death on attempted entry. Births inside shelter have latency zero, are reported separately and are excluded from the primary mean. The primary difference is fresh minus inherited mean latency. Calculate each arm and the paired difference over all outside births, with each independent family as one uncertainty cluster. For world numerator x, count n and ratio r = sum(x)/sum(n), use the sample SD of x − r n, divided by sqrt(number of worlds) and the mean n. For the paired difference use the paired world numerator. For survival use all four assigned recipients and the paired world difference. Gate for each lineage: the latency difference must exceed two paired standard errors and survival (inherited minus fresh) must be at least minus two paired standard errors. Only a pass in all three lineages establishes the specified milestone. Otherwise report failure, including the sign and uncertainty of each estimate. No change to the gate after viewing results.

Report arrivals/outside births, survival/all assigned, food minus poison and cold shelter occupancy among all assigned cold agent-turns, along with outdoor cold exposures, donor deaths and evidence that acquired weights reached newborns. Birth-to-entry can precede cold. Even a pass could reflect changed foraging or exploration. Identifying shelter-specific content requires a separate matched archive control.

Use Python from the project's environment, a committed clean scientific source and the following exact runner arguments. All three final seed values are fresh. Source file SHA-256 digests for A0, A1 and A2 respectively are `477d9d03c713dcab8072349ff9155813e5a384d3709eff67595a342b55111f53`, `35196cd30c2de5c1ed5bb9e23ce445ff6ad13bd1a33bdbf7a77f54d53edce899` and `f299069753636a303f6ba7f91be78099e8491b20a7511bdcef47fd8b4c5bbaa6`.

```powershell
python scripts/run_experiment.py --eval-protocol cold-donor-v1 --name E117-benchmark --condition-split development --seed 42 --generations 0 --eval-worlds 4 --start file:runs/20261112-023245_E103-A-blank-s0/genome_final.npy
python scripts/run_experiment.py --eval-protocol cold-donor-v1 --name E117-A0 --condition-split final --seed 2026112101 --generations 0 --eval-worlds 256 --start file:runs/20261112-023245_E103-A-blank-s0/genome_final.npy
python scripts/run_experiment.py --eval-protocol cold-donor-v1 --name E117-A1 --condition-split final --seed 2026112102 --generations 0 --eval-worlds 256 --start file:runs/20261112-050319_E103-A-blank-s1/genome_final.npy
python scripts/run_experiment.py --eval-protocol cold-donor-v1 --name E117-A2 --condition-split final --seed 2026112103 --generations 0 --eval-worlds 256 --start file:runs/20261112-102755_E103-A-blank-s2/genome_final.npy
```

Benchmark first on development worlds at full life length. Run one guarded below-normal laptop process at a time. Wall caps are 600 seconds for the benchmark and 9,900 seconds for each final invocation, subject to eight hours' cumulative compute per day. Preserve incomplete runs. Before interpreting any final result, finish the three panels and independently reconstruct the outcomes and paired uncertainties from the raw family events. Update the README and results index with the verdict.

### E118 preregistration · 21 November 2026 · Can selection evolve faster shelter finding?

The owner explicitly requested that the genomes evolve. This design was fixed before reading any E117 result. It tests whether ten generations of selection for living shelter arrival improve each of the three independently evolved A genomes. Preserve the three original parents. Use the genotype after generation ten without selecting a checkpoint. Keep food, poison, cold exposure, costly movement, within-life plasticity, and the programmed persistence trait active throughout both training and testing in the default ecology grid (movement cost 0.01, cold cost 0.02). Each life starts from its own freshly developed wiring with an empty archive. This experiment measures inherited changes in the 155-number genome. E117 separately measures transfer of acquired weight changes.

Use the existing rank-based antithetic evolution strategy, population 16, four new training worlds per generation, paired positive/negative perturbations, noise scale 0.05 and learning rate 0.02. Measure each initially outside creature's first turn ending alive inside shelter, with 301 for death or nonarrival by turn 300. Training rewards are 301 minus that turn, averaged over outside births per world; a world with no outside births contributes zero. There is no shelter-entry reward for death on arrival. Selection uses training worlds only. Keep all eleven genomes per lineage, from the unchanged parent through the ten updates, plus the training records.

On 128 independent unseen worlds for each original lineage, alternate food rules, match the parent and descendant's environment and development random keys, and allow the different genomes to produce different wiring. Separately record births inside shelter. Pool capped first-entry times over outside births; use world-clustered ratio standard errors as for E117. The paired primary difference is parent minus descendant in turns. The survival difference is descendant minus parent including every assigned newborn. A seed passes only if its travel-time improvement exceeds two paired standard errors and the survival difference is at least minus two paired standard errors. All three must pass for a replicated improvement. Retain the previous reference if the gate fails. Report every direction and uncertainty, including negative outcomes. The gate measures this task with the fixed trait. A change in genome values is not itself a gain in ability.

A0/A1/A2 source paths and exact SHA-256 values are those registered in E117. The development run uses training seed 43 and evaluation seed 44. The three training seeds are 2026112201, 2026112202 and 2026112203. Their three evaluation seeds are 2026112301, 2026112302 and 2026112303. Use one below-normal local job at a time and fresh evaluation keys. Limit the benchmark to 900 seconds, each final panel to 3,600 seconds and all work to the existing daily compute allowance. Preserve interrupted attempts. These are the commands:

    python scripts/run_experiment.py --eval-protocol shelter-evolution-v1 --name E118-benchmark --condition-split development --seed 43 --generations 1 --pop 16 --worlds 4 --sigma 0.05 --lr 0.02 --eval-worlds 4 --start file:runs/20261112-023245_E103-A-blank-s0/genome_final.npy
    python scripts/run_experiment.py --eval-protocol shelter-evolution-v1 --name E118-A0 --condition-split final --seed 2026112201 --generations 10 --pop 16 --worlds 4 --sigma 0.05 --lr 0.02 --eval-worlds 128 --start file:runs/20261112-023245_E103-A-blank-s0/genome_final.npy
    python scripts/run_experiment.py --eval-protocol shelter-evolution-v1 --name E118-A1 --condition-split final --seed 2026112202 --generations 10 --pop 16 --worlds 4 --sigma 0.05 --lr 0.02 --eval-worlds 128 --start file:runs/20261112-050319_E103-A-blank-s1/genome_final.npy
    python scripts/run_experiment.py --eval-protocol shelter-evolution-v1 --name E118-A2 --condition-split final --seed 2026112203 --generations 10 --pop 16 --worlds 4 --sigma 0.05 --lr 0.02 --eval-worlds 128 --start file:runs/20261112-102755_E103-A-blank-s2/genome_final.npy
Council review fixed two interpretation limits before running. First living arrivals can precede cold, so report the number before cold and the fraction of assigned cold turns ending alive in shelter. Ten generations with four training worlds per generation is a bounded test of this search budget. A failed gate does not exclude a better policy or a different training budget.

### E117 result · 21 November 2026 · Donor experience reduced shelter latency in all three lineages

All three preregistered gates passed. In A0, A1 and A2 order, capped first-living-entry latency averaged 161.609 ± 4.503, 160.038 ± 4.113 and 145.233 ± 4.297 turns when fresh, and 123.213 ± 3.837, 115.431 ± 3.757 and 107.891 ± 3.276 with inherited experience. The paired latency gains were 38.397 ± 5.297, 44.606 ± 5.368 and 37.341 ± 5.376 turns. Paired all-assigned survival gains were 8.984 ± 1.202, 10.742 ± 1.518 and 6.836 ± 1.002 percentage points. Each estimate uses 256 independent world families per lineage. Arrival counts among outside births were 726/960 versus 543/960, 759/955 versus 544/955 and 793/958 versus 607/958 (inherited versus fresh). The 64, 69 and 66 births inside shelter, respectively, remained in the survival denominators. The archive changed the starting weights of all 1,024 assigned newborns in each lineage. The donors included 453, 453 and 424 individuals with living outdoor cold exposure. All original death and nonarrival records were retained.

Food minus poison also increased (inherited 12.99 ± 0.46, 14.55 ± 0.44, 11.32 ± 0.33; fresh 4.64 ± 0.28, 5.45 ± 0.33, 4.46 ± 0.23). Mean fractions of assigned cold turns spent alive inside shelter were 0.0526 ± 0.0044, 0.0542 ± 0.0041, 0.0596 ± 0.0042 when inherited, and 0.0169 ± 0.0018, 0.0180 ± 0.0020, 0.0234 ± 0.0026 when fresh. Most inherited first arrivals occurred before cold. Transfer helped successors in this ecology. The donor archive can also encode food or movement experience, so shelter-specific knowledge remains unresolved. Absolute end survival with inheritance was 12.21 ± 1.09%, 15.92 ± 1.31% and 7.71 ± 0.95%. The independently registered E118 evolution test proceeds without changing this verdict.

The separate event audit finished in 32.324 seconds and checked 982 recorded file hashes. It verified the preserved 181-family prefix from the interrupted A2 attempt and the 75 newly evaluated families, the genome and measurement-source identities, matched newborns, raw living events, archive arithmetic and all three decisions. All 768 matched families were scored once. The recovery supervisor completed in 109.772 seconds with the native Windows memory fallback used once. The recovery code is 4a271c2d03f3f5c9c5d57011e4b0523503fd0776; the original scientific source is 95a254bcbe5c35c5f8ec343e056c579d562645ac. E117 supervised compute including the final audit was approximately 1,691 seconds. Reproduce the audit with:

    python tools/audit_cold_donor.py --source-commit 95a254bcbe5c35c5f8ec343e056c579d562645ac --a2-recovery runs/20261121-181108_E117-A2-recovery

### E118 result · 21 November 2026 · Genomes evolved; the three-seed performance gate failed

The held-out comparison finished on 128 independent world families per lineage (384 total). All original parents and generation-ten descendants used the same persistence rule, empty archive, ecology and development/world random streams within each paired evaluation. Outside births numbered 483, 477 and 470 of 512 assigned newborns per arm. All three originals completed ten ES updates; the independent checker reconstructed each update from its candidates, fitness, perturbations and optimizer state. Eleven saved genome vectors per lineage underlie the figure. Genome displacement measures movement in raw parameter space.

In A0/A1/A2 order the parent mean capped latency was 141.990 ± 5.817, 157.704 ± 5.870 and 147.343 ± 5.648 turns. Descendants scored 148.615 ± 5.957, 166.187 ± 6.142 and 142.353 ± 5.773. Paired turns saved were −6.625 ± 6.049, −8.482 ± 6.383 and +4.989 ± 6.901. None exceeded two paired standard errors. End survival changes were 0.000 ± 0.920, −0.586 ± 1.223 and +0.391 ± 1.073 percentage points. All three survival safeguards passed. Parent living arrivals were 317/483, 281/477 and 296/470; descendants reached 295/483, 263/477 and 301/470. Of the descendant arrivals, 275, 239 and 281 occurred before cold. Descendant cold occupancy over all assigned cold turns was 0.02979 ± 0.00411, 0.01986 ± 0.00340 and 0.03145 ± 0.00479; corresponding parent values were 0.02669 ± 0.00367, 0.02949 ± 0.00500 and 0.03332 ± 0.00458.

The registered replicated improvement gate failed. Preserve the original parents as references and all final descendants as evidence. This outcome bounds the tested budget and selection procedure. It does not overturn E117's paired benefit from acquired donor weights. A matched scrambled archive would address whether the contents of transferred experience matter; no follow-on scientific comparison is authorized by this result alone.

### E119 preregistration · 21 November 2026 · Can an evolving descendant use inherited experience to beat its own parent?

The owner requested local training of all three lineages with acquired experience transmitted across evolutionary generations. No E119 worlds have been evaluated. Start with the actual generation-ten endpoints of E118, even though that experiment failed its improvement gate. Retain the original references. Restore each endpoint's complete evolution-strategy state, including its genome, Adam moments, generation counter and RNG key. The three checkpoint SHA-256 digests in A0/A1/A2 order are 2e1bea556060085e34c2dc7cf027f639d3f36b4ee592c64b1135e8c09907a4d2, 1984cacb45e87a3fdc29bb03bb83a424873f333619008086ca98b0c768a2e78e and d0c8ce66eba91dba04151b70347ba7eae084ee9e35616ed2dd43d0a81883a740. The development run continues the single E118 benchmark update, using checkpoint 9c8fa76b3b2b3da22707199cf00988621d873f5901dcd45c7115354b87286cde. E118 had no inherited archive, so the starting archive is empty for every lineage.

Continue ten evolutionary updates under the same shelter objective, world, plasticity, persistence, population, noise scale, learning rate and four training worlds as E118. Before each update, the current population-mean parent lives on four additional independent training-only donor worlds, with four agents in each and both food rules represented equally. Each donor starts with its own genome-developed wiring and the archive that parent inherited. For each sensory-to-output connection, sum its last living strength minus its actual starting strength over the 16 donors and divide by the number that possessed it. Include zero changes and deaths. Add the resulting change to the parent's archive and clip to the existing weight bound. Each offspring candidate receives that new archive on the connections it possesses, with the same clipping. Keep all other newborn state fresh. Selection scores earlier living shelter arrival in the registered E118 manner. Save all eleven local genome/optimizer/archive states and all ten donor updates per lineage. The four added donor worlds supply experience; their outcomes do not directly determine selection. No evaluation-world experience enters training or the archive.

After the ten updates, evaluate the final descendant (global generation 20) against its immediate parent (generation 19) on 128 fresh world families per lineage. The parent receives the archive it actually inherited at birth and the descendant receives its own. Use paired world/development random keys, alternating food rules, fresh newborns and no sharing across evaluation families. Also evaluate the parent with the descendant's latest archive, the descendant with the previous archive, the descendant with an empty archive, and the original E118 endpoint with its actual empty archive. These six matched arms distinguish the complete descendant from its immediate parent and its founder, and quantify how much the trained descendant benefits from inherited weights or just the latest archive update. They do not isolate the effect of using archives during training versus an equally trained no-inheritance line, or identify what information the archive contains.

The primary measure is the parent minus descendant difference in pooled time to the first turn ending alive in shelter. Exclude inside births from latency only, count death/nonarrival as 301, and include everyone in end survival. Use world-level paired ratio standard errors for latency and paired world standard errors for survival. Each lineage passes only if the latency improvement is strictly greater than two paired standard errors and descendant-minus-parent survival is at least minus two paired standard errors. All three must pass for the replicated parent-beating claim. Report the identical contrast against the E118 founder separately, regardless of the primary outcome. For the empty-archive, previous-archive and matched-latest-archive comparisons report paired latency and survival with their uncertainty. A gain against the founder or an archive control cannot substitute for a failed immediate-parent comparison. Do not choose between checkpoints using held-out worlds.

### E119 result · 21 November 2026 · Inheritance worked mechanically; descendants failed the parent comparison

All three E118 local endpoints continued through global generation 20 on the laptop. Each of the 30 updates changed all 155 genome coordinates and all 774 entries of the sensory-to-output archive. Each lineage used 160 assigned training donors. A0/A1/A2 donor deaths totaled 160, 156 and 159; their last living weights contributed under the registered rule. In each lineage all 2,560 candidate births received a changed starting weight. Future generations inherited the recorded training archive; held-out worlds were used only after training. The independent audit verified 611 file hashes, reconstructed all ES and archive transitions, checked prior E118 world-key separation and recovered the paired estimates from the saved events.

On 128 new world families per lineage, parent and child mean capped shelter times were 198.549 ± 5.995 and 200.647 ± 6.310 turns (A0), 178.496 ± 5.736 and 219.246 ± 5.681 (A1), and 172.279 ± 5.777 and 165.975 ± 5.949 (A2). Paired parent-minus-child turns saved were −2.098 ± 3.139, −40.750 ± 6.284 and +6.304 ± 6.017. Zero of three passed the latency threshold. Paired descendant-minus-parent all-assigned survival changes were 0.000 ± 0.000, −0.391 ± 0.784 and +0.195 ± 0.339 percentage points. All relative survival safeguards passed. Parent versus child survivors were 0/512 versus 0/512, 8/512 versus 6/512 and 3/512 versus 4/512. Parent versus child living arrivals from outside were 193/479 versus 188/479, 242/476 versus 165/476, and 240/477 versus 255/477. All deaths and missed arrivals retained their full registered penalty.

Against the original E118 generation-ten founder, the child's paired turns saved were −34.927 ± 7.080, −62.216 ± 7.262 and −20.847 ± 7.427; survival changed by −3.320 ± 0.753, −4.688 ± 1.263 and −2.930 ± 1.060 percentage points. With genotype fixed at generation twenty, the full archive versus an empty archive yielded −47.969 ± 8.085, −56.433 ± 8.062 and −23.532 ± 6.991 turns saved, and survival changes of −2.539 ± 0.725, −4.492 ± 1.089 and −0.586 ± 0.648 percentage points. Thus the complete archive delayed shelter finding in all three. With genotype fixed at the descendant, the latest versus previous archive yielded −2.958 ± 2.922, −30.626 ± 6.315 and −2.153 ± 5.830 turns saved. With the latest archive fixed, the child versus parent genotype yielded −3.117 ± 2.956, +2.968 ± 4.714 and −4.379 ± 5.488. All six arm means, paired survival effects and descriptive endpoints are available in the run summaries and independent report.

Decision: the preregistered three-lineage improvement gate failed. Keep the existing reference genomes and preserve all descendants. This experiment establishes a performance cost from the final archive on this task. It does not determine why the accumulated archive behaved differently from E117's one-cohort transfer or whether a different transfer rule would help.

### Owner research direction · 21 November 2026 · Observational learning

During E119 the owner requested a direction informed by human evolution. The ongoing experiment kept its registered phenotype, archive transfer and scoring. PLAN.md records the next proposed behavioral comparison, followed by a possible multi-cohort test if observational learning passes. These proposals have no scientific result yet. They treat human evolution as a source of testable questions and preserve the distinction between copying weights and acquiring a skill through observation.

### E120 preregistration · 21 November 2026 · Does watching an experienced agent improve a newborn's first choice alone?

The owner approved the planned observational-learning test on the laptop across three independent lineages. Test the original E103 A0, A1 and A2 reference genomes as saved. This first test asks whether their existing learning rules already use public behavior. There is no genome optimization, hand-written imitation reward or weight archive. Source file SHA-256 values in order are 477d9d03c713dcab8072349ff9155813e5a384d3709eff67595a342b55111f53, 35196cd30c2de5c1ed5bb9e23ce445ff6ad13bd1a33bdbf7a77f54d53edce899 and f299069753636a303f6ba7f91be78099e8491b20a7511bdcef47fd8b4c5bbaa6.

In each independently drawn family a single same-genome demonstrator develops its own wiring and lives one ordinary 300-turn food/poison life, with the stationary hidden food rule alternating by family. Its first 150 turns provide practice. Draw the newborn independently. It passively watches the last 150 pre-action frames in the standard local visual format. The external camera is one cell south of the demonstrator's preceding position on the toroidal grid. A living demonstrator can be seen as an occupant and can emit its usual signal. Retain all families including teachers that die early, emit no useful demonstration, or make mistakes. Save teacher outcomes during practice and demonstration and the number of living visible frames. Teacher experience means opportunity to learn; competence is measured.

The observer's own energy stays at its birth value during viewing, its reward input is zero, and its own action feedback is stay. It cannot eat or share a physical life with the teacher. The movie contains visible items, occupancy and signal. The observer never receives the rule label, the teacher's energy, food/poison outcome, internal weights or sampled action commands. Its own weights, predictor and neural activity can change while watching. Transfer those own states to a fresh solo body on a new layout with the same hidden rule. There is exactly one agent throughout the 300-turn solo test. Match newborn wiring and solo world/action RNG across all four branches. Subsequent actions may diverge.

The informative branch watches the actual movie. The yoked branch gets the same frames, activity and length, with the two item-color channels exchanged for half the families under each true rule (family index divided by two, integer quotient modulo two). Thus the displayed color mapping has no consistent relationship to the solo rule. In half the families these two branches intentionally coincide. The scene-only control sees the true item movie with demonstrator occupancy and signal removed; moving scenery may still carry information. The no-demonstration control receives blank visual frames for the same number of brain updates. Keep ordinary nonvisual constants matched in all four.

The primary measure is whether the newborn's first contact with an item in the fresh solo world is food. Score one for food, zero for poison or no meal. The denominator is all assigned newborns. Record these three categories separately, full-life food minus poison and end survival. Use each independent family as the paired unit. Report means and sample-SD-over-square-root-of-family-count standard errors, using paired within-family differences for each contrast. Each lineage passes only if the informative branch beats each of the three controls by strictly more than two paired standard errors on the primary endpoint, and its all-assigned survival difference against each control is at least minus two paired standard errors. All three lineages must pass for the replicated claim. No first-meal or demonstrator-success selection. Do not equate a failed control comparison with proof that social learning is impossible.

Use 128 fresh families per lineage with masters 2026112601, 2026112602 and 2026112603. Use seed 47 on four full-size families for the benchmark. Each run uses the original foraging configuration with one individual, no ecology add-on and no persistence override. Use the tracked runner from clean committed code. The external monitor allows 900 seconds for the benchmark and 3600 seconds per final panel, with one below-normal local job, D-drive scratch, the existing runtime memory/disk safeguards and eight-hour daily allowance. Verify all three panels from saved events before interpreting the gate. Keep the README, run artifacts, commits and tags local during the owner's VPN hold.

    python scripts/run_experiment.py --eval-protocol social-learning-v1 --name E120-benchmark --condition-split development --seed 47 --generations 0 --eval-worlds 4 --start file:runs/20261112-023245_E103-A-blank-s0/genome_final.npy
    python scripts/run_experiment.py --eval-protocol social-learning-v1 --name E120-A0 --condition-split final --seed 2026112601 --generations 0 --eval-worlds 128 --start file:runs/20261112-023245_E103-A-blank-s0/genome_final.npy
    python scripts/run_experiment.py --eval-protocol social-learning-v1 --name E120-A1 --condition-split final --seed 2026112602 --generations 0 --eval-worlds 128 --start file:runs/20261112-050319_E103-A-blank-s1/genome_final.npy
    python scripts/run_experiment.py --eval-protocol social-learning-v1 --name E120-A2 --condition-split final --seed 2026112603 --generations 0 --eval-worlds 128 --start file:runs/20261112-102755_E103-A-blank-s2/genome_final.npy

### E121 preregistration · 21 November 2026 · Can the public demonstration reveal the food rule?

The council selected this follow-up before any E120 outcome was opened. E120 tests the existing newborns. E121 separately tests whether a fixed reader can recover the hidden rule from publicly visible behavior in the same kind of movie. Use independent new teacher worlds and birth keys, the three original E103 genomes, the same 150 practice and 150 public turns, identical camera and matched color controls. No observer or decoder is trained on the final films. The benchmark uses seed 48 and four families; the final masters are 2026112701, 2026112702 and 2026112703, 128 balanced families each.

The fixed tracking decoder locates the one visible teacher in consecutive frames. With a camera one cell south of its previous position, the teacher's offset in the second frame plus the south offset equals its displacement since the first frame. Use this to locate the teacher's destination within the first visible item patch. When both sightings exist and the destination contains a visible item A or B, count one vote for that color. Use frame pairs within the movie only. Predict the most often contacted color as food. Ties and no votes receive half credit, the expected score of guessing. Include every family. No hidden agent position, teacher outcome, private state or target label is supplied to the decoder. Score predictions against rule labels only after prediction. Run exactly the same reader on color-yoked movies. All no-demonstration movies yield no votes.

A second diagnostic uses item images only, so it also works on the scene-only control. Between adjacent frames score the five allowed camera shifts by the fraction of differing item-channel entries in their overlapping patches. Accept a unique minimum, otherwise abstain on that shift. Consecutive accepted shifts locate the teacher's implied current position and next move within the first patch. Count visible destination items and score majority votes using the same tie rule. This measures information potentially present in camera motion after the teacher sprite is removed. Report both readers, vote counts, no-vote and tie fractions, expected accuracy, and paired control contrasts with family-level standard errors. A failure of these readers cannot establish that every possible decoder would fail.

The usable-channel gate uses the visible-agent tracking reader. For each lineage at least half of all families must produce one or more votes, expected accuracy must be at least 60%, accuracy minus 50% must exceed two family standard errors, and accuracy minus the yoked reader must exceed two paired standard errors. All three lineages must pass. This is a property of the stimulus and reader, not a claim of social learning by an agent. If spontaneous learning fails and this channel passes, prioritize selection for the newborn's own use of observation. If this channel fails, first establish a controlled demonstration that makes a genuine choice reliably observable. If observational learning passes, test whether a subsequent cohort can independently use what an earlier observer demonstrates. Register the next intervention separately before using its evaluation worlds.

Run locally and sequentially after E120, with the existing resource floors and daily compute allowance. No model selection or tuning on E120 or E121 evaluation worlds. Use at most 900 seconds for the full-size setting benchmark and 3600 seconds for each final invocation. Keep all publication local.

    python scripts/run_experiment.py --eval-protocol social-channel-v1 --name E121-benchmark --condition-split development --seed 48 --generations 0 --eval-worlds 4 --start file:runs/20261112-023245_E103-A-blank-s0/genome_final.npy
    python scripts/run_experiment.py --eval-protocol social-channel-v1 --name E121-A0 --condition-split final --seed 2026112701 --generations 0 --eval-worlds 128 --start file:runs/20261112-023245_E103-A-blank-s0/genome_final.npy
    python scripts/run_experiment.py --eval-protocol social-channel-v1 --name E121-A1 --condition-split final --seed 2026112702 --generations 0 --eval-worlds 128 --start file:runs/20261112-050319_E103-A-blank-s1/genome_final.npy
    python scripts/run_experiment.py --eval-protocol social-channel-v1 --name E121-A2 --condition-split final --seed 2026112703 --generations 0 --eval-worlds 128 --start file:runs/20261112-102755_E103-A-blank-s2/genome_final.npy

### E120 development record · 21 November 2026 · Reward-only reference fixture

Before the first benchmark, one of seven backend checks failed. The test expected the hand-built reward-only genome to change synaptic weights during passive viewing. Its modulator takes only the newborn's energy change, which is exactly zero during every demonstration. The correct expectation is unchanged synaptic weights for that genome. The fixture now distinguishes that case from a synthetic surprise-sensitive genome whose weights can change on the same movie. The scientific code, controls, sources and registered thresholds are unchanged. No final worlds had been opened. The first backend test invocation used 40.779 seconds of guarded wall time.

### E120 result · 21 November 2026 · Watching an experienced forager failed the first-choice test in every lineage

E120 asked whether a newborn could use another agent's experience when choosing its first meal alone in a fresh layout. The 128 assigned families per original lineage included all deaths and missed meals. The observed newborns chose food first in 67/128, 66/128 and 64/128 lives, or 52.34 ± 4.43%, 51.56 ± 4.43% and 50.00 ± 4.44%. Each observer reached at least one item. The demonstrators themselves chose food on 82.86 ± 0.90%, 81.13 ± 1.04% and 81.76 ± 0.83% of late encounters. They were visible for 130.05 ± 4.44, 128.26 ± 4.56 and 136.18 ± 3.74 of 150 possible demonstration turns. Errors are one world-family standard error. Teacher accuracy pools encounters with uncertainty clustered by family.

Paired observed-minus-control differences in first-food probability, in percentage points, follow in A0/A1/A2 order. Against the color-yoked movie they were −1.56 ± 3.50, +3.91 ± 3.58 and −2.34 ± 3.23. Against the blank screen they were 0.00 ± 5.08, 0.00 ± 5.08 and +0.78 ± 5.71. Against scenery alone they were +8.59 ± 4.15, +0.78 ± 4.51 and +3.91 ± 5.13. A0 passed the scenery-only comparison. No lineage beat all three controls. End survival in the observed arm was 77.34 ± 3.71%, 82.03 ± 3.41% and 68.75 ± 4.11%. A2 also missed the survival safeguard against the yoked control (−8.59 ± 3.33 points). Survival gains against blank viewing alone cannot establish that the newborn learned the teacher's food rule.

Decision: 0/3 lineages passed. Keep all original genomes. Own synaptic weights changed while watching, but this measure and competent teachers did not predict successful first choices. Proceed to the prospectively registered E121 reader on independent movies. That test distinguishes a readable visible choice from a learner that uses it. Confirmed transfer across generations remains a separate question.

The frozen source was 839846f57186174a91bc33abe901fdab3b04a96f. Eight backend and seven runner tests passed after the synthetic fixture correction. The independent verifier checked 411 file hashes and reconstructed movies, first-meal events, comparisons and gates. It checked state transfer through fingerprints. It did not replay neural dynamics. Final process wall times including their guards were 30.854, 62.442 and 70.141 seconds. The final audit took 11.904 seconds after a preflight stopped at the newly generated results index; none of the scientific sources had changed. [Full independent reconstruction](../results/diagnostics/E120-independent-audit.json)

### E121 result · 21 November 2026 · The hidden food color was readable in new movies from all three lineages

E121 asked whether publicly visible approaches contain usable information about food. On 128 new balanced families per lineage, the tracking reader scored 84.375 ± 2.337%, 84.375 ± 2.465% and 89.844 ± 1.950% expected accuracy. A tie or missing vote earned half credit. Coverage of at least one vote was 77.344 ± 3.715%, 81.250 ± 3.463% and 84.375 ± 3.222%. The mean number of visible item contacts per assigned family was 13.984 ± 0.875, 14.320 ± 0.936 and 14.695 ± 0.907. Gains over reading the color-yoked movie were +34.375 ± 4.497, +35.938 ± 4.537 and +42.969 ± 4.531 paired percentage points. All three lineages cleared the four preregistered criteria. These uncertainties are family-level standard errors.

Using only item images, the separate scene reader scored 83.984 ± 2.476%, 83.594 ± 2.548% and 87.891 ± 2.270%. Because the camera follows the demonstrator, its route shifts the scenery. Hiding the body and its signal therefore preserves information. This result establishes that the movies contain a usable cue, while E120 measured whether the original newborns used it to choose their first food. It does not establish the necessity of seeing the teacher sprite, or that the observer brains acquired this engineered reader.

Decision: the usable-channel gate passed 3/3. Follow the preselected branch and train the observer on the outcome required when the teacher leaves. Keep the teacher genome fixed. The runner and both independent readers used source 4274b0f2b43558e6b3549de9cc48ef028ccacbc5. All 16 focused tests passed. Final guarded invocations took 126.463, 64.611 and 89.011 seconds. Independent reconstruction checked 415 file hashes, verified key separation, and recovered the control movies, votes, uncertainty and gate in 89.792 seconds. [Full independent reconstruction](../results/diagnostics/E121-independent-audit.json)

### E122 preregistration · 21 November 2026 · Select the observer for its first independent choice

E120 failed in all three lineages and E121's public-information gate passed in all three. The previously chosen branch changes what selection rewards while preserving the E120 movie, camera, passive neural updates and solo foraging world. The original E103 A0, A1 and A2 genomes and hashes pinned in E120 are the starting observers. Each lineage's teacher stays at its original genome throughout. A practiced teacher contributes public behavior only. During selection and test the learner acts alone for up to 300 steps in a new layout after 150 frames of observation. No architecture additions or inherited teacher weights.

Attempt ten antithetic evolution-strategy rounds per lineage. Each round uses 16 candidates, four fresh balanced-rule families, sigma 0.05, Adam learning rate 0.02 and L2 coefficient 0.005 on informative rounds. Start with zero optimizer moments. Share the fixed teacher's film and solo world across candidates, and the same pupil birth keys for each antithetic candidate pair. Fitness is the fraction of assigned solo lives whose first meal was food. Poison or no meal scores zero. Selecting absolute success avoids rewarding a change that merely harms the color-yoked control. Assign equal candidate scores their average rank. When all candidates tie, consume the round's keys but leave the genome, moments and update counter unchanged. Save all ten attempted rounds, candidate evidence, teachers, key assignments, genomes and informative update count. Freeze the endpoint after the last attempted round regardless of its training score.

Evaluate five branches on 128 fresh families per lineage: the evolved observer under the intact, color-yoked, scenery-only and blank movies, and its original observer under the identical intact movie. Match solo worlds, action random streams and pupil development keys. The teacher and original observer have the same encoded genome but independent births. Retain every family. Report arm means and one family-level standard error for first food, end survival and fitness. For each comparator use paired within-family differences. A lineage passes only if the evolved observed arm beats each comparator by strictly more than two paired standard errors in first-food probability, and meets survival difference ≥ minus two paired standard errors against each. All three lineages must pass. Also report all control comparisons and informative rounds when this intersection fails. The benchmark uses a single attempted round, four training families and four evaluation families. Calibration and test data remain separate; the final panels never update the genome.

Training masters are 2026112801, 2026112802 and 2026112803; held-out masters are 2026112901, 2026112902 and 2026112903. Development uses 49 and 50 respectively. Run only at low priority on the laptop from committed code. Cap the development invocation at 900 seconds and each final at 3600 seconds. Preserve the one-job, disk, committed-memory and daily resource limits. Independently reconstruct the genetic updates and held-out decisions. If the gate fails, test whether an explicit, calibrated two-color belief can help the original observer use the demonstrated information, with an otherwise identical belief-reset control. If it succeeds, independently replicate and erase the successful observer's acquired state to test retention. Register the selected experiment separately.

### E122 result · 21 November 2026 · Selection changed the genomes but failed the observer comparison

The 30 attempted evolutionary rounds all contained variation in candidate fitness and all produced an optimizer update. Each of the 155 genome coordinates changed at every update. The teachers stayed fixed. All descendants were tested on their registered 128 fresh families. Descendants chose food first in 49.22 ± 4.44%, 53.13 ± 4.43% and 51.56 ± 4.43% of assigned solo lives. Under the same intact movies and solo worlds, the starting observers scored 53.91 ± 4.42%, 46.09 ± 4.42% and 45.31 ± 4.42%. The paired changes due to training were −4.69 ± 4.69, +7.03 ± 4.32 and +6.25 ± 5.84 percentage points. Each uncertainty is one family-level standard error.

In A0/A1/A2 order, the observed descendants' first-food advantages in percentage points were −1.56 ± 4.00, +0.78 ± 3.23 and +7.03 ± 3.71 against color-yoked movies; +0.78 ± 5.02, +4.69 ± 5.19 and +4.69 ± 5.19 against blank viewing; and +1.56 ± 4.57, +7.03 ± 4.46 and +3.13 ± 4.70 against scenery alone. None of the four first-food comparisons passed in any lineage. A1 also missed the survival safeguard against the color-yoked control (−7.03 ± 3.36 points). All held-out comparisons include the full 128 assigned families per lineage.

Decision: the improvement gate failed 0/3 under this ten-round budget and absolute-first-food objective. The genomes genuinely evolved; the independent behavioral comparison remains the test of useful change. Preserve the descendants and switch strategies. E123 will test an explicit rule estimate calibrated on the original pupils' own outcomes, together with a causal reset before the pupil acts. This decision does not use any E122 evaluation sample for calibration. The fixed teachers and public-film design from E120 remain the references.

### E123 preregistration · 21 November 2026 · Can a calibrated belief from public behavior guide what the newborn does alone?

E120 and E122 failed the learner gate; E121 established a readable public channel in all three lineages. Implement one engineered connection between social evidence and action, starting from the original E103 A0/A1/A2 foragers and their original genome hashes recorded in E120. Keep their existing development, predictor, synaptic updates and action feedback. There is no genome optimization. The research inspiration is the use of a learned task belief as an input to a policy, the use of the learner's own experience to interpret action-free demonstrations, and recall after an expert departs. This narrow adaptation is separate from reproducing those papers' algorithms. [VariBAD](https://jmlr.csail.mit.edu/papers/volume22/21-0657/21-0657.pdf) · [Behavioral cloning from observation](https://www.ijcai.org/proceedings/2018/0687.pdf) · [Few-shot imitation](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC10684502/)

From the 150 public frames of an independent experienced teacher, count E121 tracking-reader votes for A and B. Set z=(A−B)/max(1,A+B) and q=1/(1+exp(−lambda*z)). A missing or tied vote gives q=0.5. In the solo world the newborn adds 3*(2*q−1)*(visible A−visible B at the destination) to each of its five usual movement logits, including staying in place. The added bias is bounded by three in magnitude. Only its current ordinary neighborhood is used. Preserve the usual action sampling and feed its actual chosen move back into the brain. The scalar q stays fixed throughout that solo lifetime. During viewing the newborn receives no personal reward. The independently drawn teacher's reward, hidden rule and internal state are unavailable to inference.

Calibrate lambda separately in each lineage from 32 fresh teacher/pupil families with both rules balanced. The pupil acts alone from unassisted birth in a new layout sharing the teacher world's rule. Record the visible color it first contacted and whether its own energy rose or fell. Choose lambda from −4, −2, −1, −0.5, 0, 0.5, 1, 2, 4 to minimize mean binary log loss for that personal outcome given z and the color contacted. Use at most one observed contact per family. Retain families without one in coverage and omit them from the loss. Choose zero if none supplied a contact; on tied losses select smaller absolute lambda and then the positive sign. Store every calibration sample, every candidate loss, the chosen value and manifest hash. Write the frozen selection record before any evaluation family is accessed. The calibrated scalar is the only learned parameter in the added mechanism.

Evaluate 128 new, balanced paired families per lineage. Each full trial uses the E120 original 300-step teacher and an independently wired newborn, followed by a 300-step solo life on a fresh physical layout. Compare six arms: intact movie with calibrated belief, color-yoked movie, scenery-only movie, blank movie, intact movie with just q reset to 0.5 after viewing, and the actual unassisted original newborn from birth without the movie. In the reset comparison hold the learned W, x and Wp and every solo key fixed. For all arms record first food, first poison, no meal, end survival, fitness and first-contact latency. First food scores one and every other assigned outcome zero. A lineage passes only if intact beats each of the five comparators by strictly more than two paired family standard errors for first food and meets survival difference ≥ minus two paired standard errors for each. Require all three lineages. Report each control even on failure. Scene-only is a control for this particular tracking readout; E121 showed that a different reader can recover route information from that scenery.

Development uses calibration master 51 and evaluation master 52, four families each. Final calibration masters are 2026113001, 2026113002 and 2026113003. Held-out masters are 2026120101, 2026120102 and 2026120103. Each final invocation contains both phases with the immutable calibration boundary. Use at most 900 seconds for calibration and 3600 seconds for the entire final invocation; cap the full benchmark at 900 seconds. Run sequentially with existing laptop/disk/day safeguards. Reconstruct calibration, control transformations, all five paired contrasts and gates independently before interpretation. A confirmed positive result motivates a separately registered multi-cohort transmission comparison. A first-food gain accompanied by a survival failure motivates releasing the added bias after the first personal encounter. These are distinct decisions and cannot change the present gate.

### E123 result · 21 November 2026 · Calibrated social evidence improved the first independent food choice in three lineages

All three original lineages calibrated lambda=4 from 32/32 assigned personal contact outcomes apiece before their held-out families opened. The public teacher-choice reader and bounded action bias were engineered. The calibrated reliability and each newborn's movie-dependent estimate were acquired at different times. The 155-number genomes stayed fixed. On 128 fresh paired families per lineage, the observed newborns' first meals were food in 110, 97 and 99 lives, or 85.94 ± 3.08%, 75.78 ± 3.80% and 77.34 ± 3.71%. Observed survival was 113/128, 112/128 and 115/128, or 88.28 ± 2.85%, 87.50 ± 2.93% and 89.84 ± 2.68%. Uncertainties are one family-level standard error.

Erasing just the food-color estimate after the identical demonstration lowered first-food counts to 68, 66 and 52. Intact-minus-reset differences were +32.81 ± 4.31, +24.22 ± 4.11 and +36.72 ± 4.28 paired percentage points. Against the actual unassisted original newborn they were +29.69 ± 5.24, +26.56 ± 5.38 and +32.03 ± 5.19 points. Gains over the color-yoked movies were +33.59 ± 4.19, +28.13 ± 4.83 and +28.13 ± 4.43 points; over scenery alone +27.34 ± 4.40, +25.00 ± 4.44 and +37.50 ± 4.83; and over the blank movie +29.69 ± 5.13, +22.66 ± 5.46 and +32.03 ± 5.07. Every intact-minus-control first-food contrast exceeded two paired SE. All 15 survival safeguards passed. Survival gains versus the belief reset were +9.38 ± 3.02, +12.50 ± 3.68 and +15.63 ± 3.41 points. Complete measurements and uncertainty for all six arms are in the saved summaries and independent audit.

Decision: 3/3 lineages passed. Under a stationary two-color rule, a pupil could use public behavior to guide an independent first choice when we provided this particular route from evidence to action. The reset identifies the contribution of the added memory. This does not establish that natural selection invented it, that reading the teacher sprite was necessary, or that the effect survives a misleading teacher or a change of rule. E124 will ask whether an actual pupil can supply useful behavior to the next cohort and test cumulative improvement separately.

### E124 result · 21 November 2026 · The lesson survived three teaching handoffs; cumulative improvement failed to replicate

All 384 independent chains completed, covering 4,992 solo lifetimes across founders and matched branches. Each newborn received a fresh body, layout and independently developed brain. The successful E123 genome and scalar calibration were fixed within lineage. Only the predecessor's actual public behavior crossed cohorts. Its first-food success by cohort was 83.59 ± 3.29%, 86.72 ± 3.01%, 86.72 ± 3.01% in A0; 78.91 ± 3.62%, 90.63 ± 2.59%, 83.59 ± 3.29% in A1; and 76.56 ± 3.76%, 79.69 ± 3.57%, 86.72 ± 3.01% in A2. Each error is one standard error across 128 independent chains.

The registered retention gate passed in all three lineages. At every cohort, the predecessor-movie pupil beat its matched color-yoked and belief-reset siblings by more than two paired SE. At cohort three, the gains over the yoked control were +39.84 ± 4.34, +35.16 ± 4.38 and +35.16 ± 4.65 paired percentage points; gains over the reset were +36.72 ± 4.56, +30.47 ± 4.23 and +31.25 ± 4.40. All registered survival lower bounds exceeded the −10-point margin. This screen does not establish identical survival.

Only A2 passed the additional cumulative-improvement gate. Third minus first cohort success was +3.13 ± 3.83, +4.69 ± 4.55 and +10.16 ± 3.97 paired percentage points. At cohort three, replacing the founder's replay with the preceding pupil's public movie added +1.56 ± 3.68, +1.56 ± 3.84 and +10.16 ± 3.48 points. A0 and A1 missed the threshold on both contrasts. The three-lineage accumulation criterion failed. The two thresholds remain distinct: the data support repeated behavioral transmission on this stationary rule while leaving a generally rising trajectory unestablished.

Decision: preserve the controller and calibration. Shift to a world where the hidden rule can reverse during life. The next saved-controller measurement asks whether a once-useful lesson can be revised; no further selection is needed to measure that vulnerability. ### E125 preregistration · 21 November 2026 · What happens when a correct social lesson becomes obsolete?

E124 replicated retention while cumulative improvement passed in only one lineage. Proceed to the owner's Phase 3 rung 1 measurement from saved controllers, before any new evolution. This version changes one world property. At one hidden, randomized time during the 300-step solo life, food and poison exchange roles. The matched stationary world uses the identical initial rule, board, wiring, action keys and analysis boundary with no change in its rule. Preserve every observation channel, action, energy cost, item and lifetime budget. The controller gets no change flag. Each original lineage gets 128 fresh families. Randomly permute 16 copies of every cell of the two initial rules crossed with four flip steps (100, 125, 150, 175) using a dedicated seed-derived key. Freeze the assignment manifest before evaluating families. Development uses four full families, covering all four times and both rules. Until the boundary the paired branches must agree on every recorded world/agent trace and on the action and observation at the boundary before its reward. Save actual pre-action local inputs, sampled actions, energies, item grids, encounters and survival so that these checks and classifications can be reconstructed. No post-change target may enter the actor other than through its own normal sensory feedback.

The matched social reset keeps acquired W, x and Wp and clears only q. Use the E103, E113 and E123 calibration source hashes already pinned by the immutable source registries; use no prior evaluation families. The only new social engine option allows the existing action readout in this version of a reversal world. The old stationary and evaluation entry points retain their behavior.

Measure the first item within each of three 50-turn windows relative to the boundary: pre [−50,0), early [0,50), late [50,100). Score first food one and first poison, death or no contact zero. Keep every assigned family. Report mean/SE, no-contact probability, fitness, contact-conditioned accuracy with denominator and survival at the boundary, +50, +100 and life end. Treat end-survivor-only numbers as descriptive with their subgroup counts. Apply the same windows to the stationary copy. Let recovery be (late minus early first-food success in reversal) minus (late minus early success in the stationary copy), paired by family.

All three lineages must pass. Preserve the original gate even if performance changes immediately and the recovery contrast is flat. Report the individual conditions and every other controller separately. A pass would establish transfer under this restricted test.

### E125 result · 22 November 2026 · The lesson persisted after its food color became poisonous

We evaluated the four saved controllers in both the stationary and reversal copies of each of 384 independent families, 128 per lineage. The prepared social pupil cleared none of the five transfer conditions in any lineage. In the late 50-turn window its first-food success was 16/128 (12.50 ± 2.93%), 23/128 (17.97 ± 3.41%) and 26/128 (20.31 ± 3.57%). The same-window stationary scores were 100/128 (78.13 ± 3.67%), 89/128 (69.53 ± 4.08%) and 91/128 (71.09 ± 4.02%). Death, no contact and first poison all count as failures. The stationary-adjusted early-to-late recovery was −10.16 ± 6.45, +11.72 ± 7.81 and +5.47 ± 7.87 percentage points. Reversal reduced end survival by 61.72 ± 4.85, 72.66 ± 4.11 and 66.41 ± 4.61 paired percentage points. All errors are one family-level SE.

The memory reset identifies a cost specifically associated with retaining the original lesson. Before reversal, intact minus reset first-food success was +12.50 ± 5.43, +25.00 ± 5.66 and +22.66 ± 5.68 paired points. In the late post-change window the differences were −14.06 ± 3.96, −14.84 ± 4.85 and −10.16 ± 4.94 points. Intact minus reset end survival was −20.31 ± 5.24, −25.78 ± 5.11 and −26.56 ± 5.26 points. The newborns in each comparison retained matched acquired neural state after watching. Only the explicit belief was reset. Its stationary calibration and action bias were fixed during solo life. These measurements show a cost of the old lesson after a flip, not that all failures followed from that memory.

The unprepared original rules controllers also struggled. Their late reversal first-food rates were 23.44 ± 3.76%, 30.47 ± 4.08% and 22.66 ± 3.71%. For the matched unprepared direct recurrent controllers the rates were 0.00 ± 0.00%, 0.78 ± 0.78% and 2.34 ± 1.34%. Those controllers had been selected under the earlier stationary task. E125 tested transfer using saved parameters and cannot decide the trained architecture comparison or its lesion gate.

The corrected independent checker passed on the original development artifact and all three final panels on source 36b3ac1737a5258434ccf0fa29b5767b1aa2211f. Seven synthetic audit groups and 22 focused tests passed. The final audit checked 636 hashes, reconstructed the pre-change shared histories and the scored outcomes, and took 92.230 seconds. Per-tick neural weight matrices were not independently replayed. The complete E125 guarded work charged 1,026.076 seconds including development, interruption, correction checks and final evaluations. [Independent reconstruction](../results/diagnostics/E125-independent-audit.json)

Decision: retain E124's stationary teaching finding and record the failed E125 transfer gate. Under the owner's task ladder and council delegation, proceed to matched evolution from blank on the randomized-reversal world. Train existing plastic and direct recurrent architectures under identical exposure and measure how much post-change Hebbian learning contributes. Defer new mechanisms until the unchanged architectures have had that opportunity.

### E126 preregistration · 22 November 2026 · Can evolution from blank produce recovery after a hidden rule change?

Train two architectures in the same solitary world as E125. Each has 103 sensory inputs, 48 hidden units, six motor outputs and two neural updates per turn. The 155-parameter plastic genome grows the original rules-v0 controller. The 8,424-parameter direct-rnn-v1 genome specifies the recurrent weights directly. Initialize both from their established blank distributions. Neither group receives a social demonstration. Both face 300-turn lives, the same observations and energy costs, and a single unannounced change of food color. The comparison includes the effects of different encodings, parameter counts and birth wiring. It cannot isolate each of them.

Soltoggio and colleagues evolved controllers for mazes whose reward locations changed during a lifetime. Their neuron-specific modulation multiplied a four-term local plasticity update. Seed already has the four terms and a different, shared modulation signal. We can therefore test what its existing learning rule contributes in a changing foraging world by disabling its weight updates after the first changed outcome. The paper’s maze results do not predict the outcome in Seed. [Soltoggio et al., Artificial Life XI](https://andrea.soltoggio.net/data/papers/SoltoggioALife2008.pdf)

The direct controller has its own route to adaptation. In RL², an agent's past actions and rewards changed its recurrent state while its weights stayed fixed during deployment. Our direct controller gets Seed's sensory feedback and recurrent state under the same ES budget as the plastic controller. Either architecture can win this comparison. [Duan et al., RL²](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1611.02779)

Train three matched seed pairs for 200 attempted generations each. Each generation has 128 candidates and four new independent base worlds. Replay each base under both initial rules using shared layout, action and birth random keys. Assign the four hidden flip times 100, 125, 150 and 175 once each, permuted independently per generation. Match world and random key schedules across architectures and antithetic twins. This is eight lives per candidate. Both architectures use antithetic ES, mutation scale 0.1, Adam learning rate 0.1, L2 0.005 and average ranks for tied scores. A fully tied generation consumes its worlds and advances the random stream while preserving the genome and optimizer moments. Stop at 200 attempts regardless of how many changed the genome. Use only the terminal population mean.

For each base world and each initial rule, count food minus poison in the 50 turns from `flip+50` inclusive to `flip+100` exclusive. Use the smaller of the two net returns for that base world, then average over worlds. This order prevents consistently favoring one color from scoring well on the strength of the opposite-rule sibling. No contact after death contributes zero, so survival and a positive absolute score have separate gates. The environment's rewards are unchanged. Store keys and perturbation hashes, per-candidate event and objective evidence, and immutable attempted-generation checkpoints. Record the attempted and informative update counts separately. A continuation creates a new folder, validates its predecessor, and resumes its stored optimizer and RNG.

Lock held-out evaluation until all six endpoints are recorded. For each paired training seed, evaluate its two endpoints on 128 new independent bases, 32 per flip time. Each base is replayed with both initial rules, giving 256 lives per condition. Match layouts, developments and action randomness across stationary, reversal and intervention conditions. Each architecture runs intact in both worlds. The plastic controller also receives a lesion in both worlds. Freeze only Hebbian updates from neural tick `flip+1` onward; the first action and reward at the reversal boundary must still match the intact reversal. Keep the predictor and recurrent dynamics active. Apply the same lesion at the shadow time in stationary copies. The intact agent never sees the time label or the intervention.

The primary outcome J is average minimum late net return, using independent bases for standard errors. Rung 1 passes only if the SAME two or more of the three matched seed pairs satisfy all four conditions: plastic minus direct J is strictly greater than twice the square root of the sum of their squared SEs; plastic J is strictly greater than twice its own SE; all-assigned late first-food success is at least 0.60 and strictly more than two clustered SE above 0.50; and intact plastic J exceeds its reversal lesion by strictly more than two paired SE. Death, no contact and poison each score zero on first-food success. In every seed, at least 0.50 of bases must end with both plastic rule replays alive, and the lower two-paired-SE bound on plastic minus direct joint survival must exceed −0.10. Also report paired J differences, original whole-life fitness, window contact/no-contact outcomes and survival. Report wins, failures and indeterminate superiority separately. Preserve all comparisons regardless of direction. A stopped study leaves the rung undecided and cannot unlock a new mechanism.

Use separate three-generation development benchmarks at full population and world count for both controllers before scheduling finals. Freeze the study ceiling at 43,200 seconds across local dates. Maximum 28,800 seconds of all Seed compute per local day. Use the measured cold generation and 1.5 times the slower of the two warm generation durations, including per-process cold/setup, to project the six full endpoints. Reserve 1,800 seconds for evaluation and 1,800 for verification. Include actual benchmark charges once and check footprint against available disk. If the conservative forecast exceeds the remaining total ceiling, preserve results and bring the cost decision back to council. Tie handling, scientific targets and gates stay fixed. Run one below-normal-priority process at a time. Each final segment attempts at most 50 generations, with a 3,000-second soft worker limit and a 3,600-second full-process limit. Checkpoint every ten attempts and at each ordinary stop. Development has a 780-second soft limit and a 900-second full-process cap. The coordinator records actual wall time across midnight and preserves the disk and live memory safeguards. No source or result publication during the VPN hold.

Commands below use PowerShell arrays so every argument reaches the tracked runner explicitly. The local resource guard invokes these commands one at a time. For each target run part 001, then a new numbered part only while that target remains incomplete. Set `$previous` to the unique validated run directory of its preceding part. Interrupted attempts are preserved. Resolve any interruption before launching a continuation. The six training targets use matched seeds 2026120401–03. Held-out seeds are independent, 2026120501–03. Run evaluation only after all six endpoints reach 200.

```powershell
$python = '.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe'
$entry = 'scripts/run_experiment.py'
$common = @('--eval-protocol', 'reversal-evolution-v1', '--pop', 128, '--worlds', 4,
    '--sigma', 0.1, '--lr', 0.1, '--eval-worlds', 128, '--checkpoint-every', 10, '--start', 'blank')
$architectures = [ordered]@{ plastic = 'rules-v0'; direct = 'direct-rnn-v1' }
foreach ($label in $architectures.Keys) {
    & $python $entry @common --controller $architectures[$label] --condition-split development `
        --name "E126-benchmark-$label" --seed 2026120400 --generations 3 --max-train-seconds 780
}
# For seed index $i (0, 1, 2), $label ('direct', 'plastic'), and its next $part (1, 2, ...):
$partName = 'E126-{0}-s{1}-part{2:D3}' -f $label,$i,$part
$continuation = if ($part -gt 1) { @('--resume', $previous) } else { @() }
& $python $entry @common --controller $architectures[$label] --condition-split final `
    --name $partName --seed (2026120401+$i) --generations 200 --max-train-seconds 3000 @continuation
# After all six targets, one paired evaluation per seed index:
foreach ($i in 0..2) {
    & $python $entry --eval-protocol reversal-evolution-v1 --condition-split final `
        --name "E126-eval-s$i" --seed (2026120501+$i) --generations 0 --eval-worlds 128 --start blank
}
```

#### E127 result and gate decision · 22 November 2026

All three original E103 plastic lineages **passed local reversal, transfer to a fresh grid in the same lineage, and the local Hebbian intervention test**. Zero of three matched E113 fixed-weight recurrent lineages passed local reversal or the combined transfer gate. Each estimate uses 128 independent families per lineage, averaging the two counterbalanced initial rules within a family. Local tests also average the twelve ordered cue placements. Uncertainties below are one standard error. For the two grid arms, every assigned life counts, including no contact and death before contact. [Independent reconstruction](../results/diagnostics/E127-independent-audit.json) · [Run comparison and all measurements](../results/diagnostics/E127-comparison.json)

| Plastic lineage | Old signed preference before flip | New signed preference after 16 reversed contacts | Reversed minus stationary local preference | First food in fresh grid, reversed / stationary history | Paired first-food advantage | Local advantage from keeping Hebbian updates |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| A0 | 0.662 ± 0.006 | 0.217 ± 0.020 | 0.903 ± 0.023 | 0.621 ± 0.029 / 0.035 ± 0.011 | 0.586 ± 0.032 | 0.879 ± 0.016 |
| A1 | 0.653 ± 0.007 | 0.198 ± 0.019 | 0.929 ± 0.020 | 0.625 ± 0.032 / 0.039 ± 0.012 | 0.586 ± 0.034 | 0.847 ± 0.016 |
| A2 | 0.642 ± 0.007 | 0.223 ± 0.020 | 0.917 ± 0.023 | 0.629 ± 0.030 / 0.039 ± 0.012 | 0.590 ± 0.033 | 0.862 ± 0.018 |

The fixed-weight controls' paired first-food differences were −0.008 ± 0.018, −0.008 ± 0.012 and 0.000 ± 0.016. Keeping the original plastic controller's Hebbian updates enabled while the rule changed also improved first-food success versus freezing them by 0.566 ± 0.032, 0.562 ± 0.034 and 0.578 ± 0.033. This latter contrast was descriptive. The sizable primary grid gains reflect both success after changed feedback and a strong preference for the obsolete food in the stationary-history copies. Exposure was guided and immediate transfer used a fresh body. E125's freely encountered reversals and E126's uncompleted training comparison remain distinct. The next research question is whether this revised preference lasts through a cue-free interval.

### E128 preregistration · 22 November 2026 · Retention through an active empty-world interval

The published animal-learning decision calls for testing retention after an immediate local and grid pass. The owner delegated the choice of follow-up to the council. Keep ordinary hunger feedback, actions and lifetime plasticity. Compare the same history before and after the interval, and compare reversed against stationary feedback. Use the [frozen E128 protocol](E128_PROTOCOL.md) for all thresholds, including the all-three-lineages decision and the strict −0.05 paired lower bound. #### E128 result and gate decision · 22 November 2026

**Local and combined retention failed in all three plastic lineages.** All three still showed significant positive preference and a history effect after 33 empty turns. In every plastic lineage, the sole failed criterion in both local and grid testing was the registered margin for 33 minus zero turns. Both fixed-weight gates also failed in all three controls, which never established the required immediate preference. Absence of an immediate learned response prevents a comparison of how quickly the two architectures forget. Because the interval changes both the neural and physical state, this result does not establish which process caused the decline.

The council closes E128 at its original threshold. Its next decision is one causal diagnostic: switch off Hebbian updates only during the empty interval, leave the predictor working and restart ordinary weight updates for independent foraging. A comparison with normal learning can distinguish the effect of permitting those updates during the empty interval. Preregister a minimum rescue size and stop this retention sequence if the intervention fails. No evolved or lasting architectural improvement follows from a scheduling intervention alone.

### E129 preregistration · 22 November 2026 · A single intervention during the cue-free interval

Does allowing ongoing Hebbian weight updates during the 33 empty turns contribute to E128's decline? Use the [E129 protocol](E129_PROTOCOL.md), including its three nested, all-lineage decisions. First require a frozen-minus-normal advantage of at least 0.025 in delayed reversal-history signed preference and greater than two paired SE in **each** of the three original plastic lineages. To claim local rescue, the frozen-interval arm must also meet the unchanged E128 local thresholds. To claim transfer rescue, it must meet the E128 grid thresholds and show a direct grid advantage over the normal interval exceeding two paired SE in those same lineages. Endpoints are zero and 33 turns, with common random inputs, both acquisition histories and all assigned lives. Direct recurrent controls use ordinary dynamics throughout. This diagnostic closes the current retention investigation at its registered result.

### E130 · Preregistration · 22 November 2026

The owner asked us to work toward behaviors studied in mammals. The council selected autonomous reversal after checking that E112 already evaluated the same founding and generation-200 genomes. The new question is whether the descendants' recovery exceeds that of their own founders when both must find every item themselves. The [protocol](E130_PROTOCOL.md) fixes the outcome, comparison with stationary worlds, numerical criteria, original-assignment denominator, common random draws, fresh worlds and stopping conditions. The mouse study motivates the behavioral question. The E130 comparison uses different sensory inputs and a deterministic food rule.

Prediction: each of the three evolved lineages acquires the old rule, favors the new food late, improves through the post-switch period, and beats its founder on absolute and reversal-specific recovery while preserving stationary behavior and survival. A joint pass requires every numerical criterion in the protocol in all three lineages. A failure of any required component in any lineage leaves the joint claim unestablished. No final outcomes have been inspected. The rehearsal uses four development families, and each final panel has 64 independent families, both counterbalanced rules and both genotypes. The E103 genotype files are fixed before measurement. No new evolution or memory intervention is included.

### E131 · Preregistration · 22 November 2026

Test one architectural change to the action policy. Can an agent that spends its limited exploration after a prolonged absence of observed energy gain find the newly safe food sooner and choose it more often? Keep both a native control and an outcome-independent exploration control. The same three original E103 generation-200 genomes, ordinary brain, food worlds and observations are used throughout. The [protocol](E131_PROTOCOL.md) freezes the 12-turn counter, one move per phased 25-turn block, independently assigned control offsets, fresh development and held-out seeds, family-level estimators, survival tolerances, cost limit and stopping rule.

Pass only if adaptive exploration meets every behavioral condition against both controls in all three lineages plus its rehearsal cost criterion. Record each component when a gate misses. A separate architecture critic identified a possible false win from lower stationary performance. The registration therefore requires **both** direct late reversal superiority and an improvement adjusted for stationary differences. A death or missed food receives a waiting time of 50, and all originally assigned animals contribute. No future-schedule input, hidden item label, new generation or parameter search is permitted.

| [A2](../runs/20261122-201521_E131-A2) | −0.070 ± 0.034 | +3.42 ± 1.10 / +1.77 ± 0.99 | −0.051 ± 0.047 / −0.016 ± 0.048 | +0.063 ± 0.063 / +0.033 ± 0.070 |

Each cell with two values shows the ordinary control first and the equal-quota control second. Each ± is one family standard error, paired for contrasts. Late Q assigns food +1, poison −1, and death or no contact 0. First-food latency counts every assigned creature, with 50 for no food in the early window. The adaptive warm median was 0.576824 s, ordinary 0.436437 s and equal quota 0.486673 s. The adaptive/ordinary ratio of 1.321666 met the 1.5 ceiling.

A2 alone passed the latency contrast against ordinary. No lineage passed latency against equal quota. A1 alone cleared the direct and stationary-adjusted late Q gains against equal quota. All three adaptive late scores were negative, and every stationary late-Q safeguard missed the registered −0.05 lower-bound margin. Both survival safeguards passed against both controls for A2 and against quota for A1. Absolute reversal outcomes, realized overrides and all safeguard bounds are in the [comparison](../results/diagnostics/E131-comparison.json). The guards and audit checked complete live-block quotas. Variable duration of partial blocks means realized dose can differ.

**Decision.** The behavioral gate failed at 0/3 and the setting fails adoption. Retire this 12-turn exploration setting from the improvement sequence. No tuning or extra panels on these worlds. The distinct next question is whether balanced, voluntarily chosen item encounters improve feedback access and reversal recovery against a matched sham. This is a candidate for separate preregistration.

**Verification and compute.** Windows CIM access stopped the first readiness attempt before Python started. Its 1.296 seconds remained in the canonical ledger. The guarded retry passed 36 focused tests and seven independent synthetic check groups. The four-family development panel and its audit took 49.520 and 22.799 seconds. Final panels took 190.611, 104.387 and 147.074 seconds serially, with 36.712 seconds for the joint audit. Both compiled and warm policy timings are in the development record. The single [hosted Windows job](https://github.com/Winter-Soren/seed/actions/runs/45034835612) passed 601 tests in 1,096.658 test seconds and used 1,195 whole-job seconds. Study charge was 1,787.798/5,400 seconds, including reporting. The shared daily ledger stood at 22,891.011/28,800 seconds. The reporting check preserved all earlier diagnostic JSON bytes. ### E132 · Preregistration · 22 November 2026

E131's independent audit found zero of three lineages passed the registered behavioral criteria. The council recommended separating access to personally sampled evidence from its use by the brain. The alternative was a local gate on weight updates. Faster arrival at one food cannot distinguish those causes. First compare voluntary nearby presentations with sham presentations of the same two items at random distances. Both starting rules, stationary continuations and a fresh solo probe help distinguish changes of preference from habitual choices in the apparatus. The [protocol](E132_PROTOCOL.md) freezes what counts as exposure, how to handle deaths and the success criteria.

Pass only if all three fixed E103 lineages gain access to both colors and pass the old/new preference checks, direct and stationary-adjusted solo comparisons and survival safeguards. A failed manipulation leaves the underlying explanation open. No E132 measurement has been made at registration.

& $python $entry @common --condition-split final --name E132-A0 --seed 2026121101 --eval-worlds 64 --start file:runs/20261112-023245_E103-A-blank-s0/genome_final.npy

& $python $entry @common --condition-split final --name E132-A2 --seed 2026121103 --eval-worlds 64 --start file:runs/20261112-102755_E103-A-blank-s2/genome_final.npy
