**Before measurement, 22 November.** The independent reviewer asked which of two adjacent observations defines the named survival safeguard. End-of-exposure survival is measured after the final presentation tick (128 ticks). Report survival after the empty feedback tick (129) separately. Only animals alive at that second observation enter the local and solo readouts. This distinction was frozen before tests or development measurement.

#### E132 result · 22 November 2026

The [four-family rehearsal](../runs/20261122-204850_E132-benchmark) exercised living and dead readout paths. All 43 focused tests and five independent synthetic checks passed. The [development audit](../results/diagnostics/E132-development-audit.json) passed. The [forecast](../results/diagnostics/E132-development-forecast.json) allowed all three panels. Actual guarded runtimes were 46.24, 54.27 and 103.25 seconds for [A0](../runs/20261122-205421_E132-A0), [A1](../runs/20261122-205527_E132-A1) and [A2](../runs/20261122-205649_E132-A2). Each stayed below its 500-second cap. The independent final audit checked 192 families and 218 file hashes in 42.45 seconds. Source `4f3bdb772dc6cf92dacf9b06e48af3b0873933b0` served every panel. Local study time including readiness, rehearsal, audits, forecasting and reporting was 322.83 of 3,600 seconds. Earlier diagnostic files retained identical bytes. [Audit](../results/diagnostics/E132-independent-audit.json) · [Historical check](../results/diagnostics/E132-historical-diagnostics-proof.json)

The table reports nearby minus random-distance offers, paired within families, with one standard error. One signed solo-score unit is the difference between the probabilities of first finding food and first finding poison. Each lineage had 64 independent families, each replaying both initial rules. Every originally assigned animal contributes.

| Lineage | Both colors sampled, points | Reversal exposure survival, points | Reversal solo-score gain | Reversal gain minus stationary gain | Absolute local new-food preference after nearby offers |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| A0 | +71.1 ± 3.8 | −16.4 ± 3.2 | +0.234 ± 0.093 | +0.023 ± 0.146 | −0.003 ± 0.012 |
| A1 | +68.0 ± 3.9 | −13.3 ± 3.2 | +0.188 ± 0.082 | +0.023 ± 0.133 | +0.020 ± 0.011 |
| A2 | +72.7 ± 4.0 | −14.8 ± 3.3 | +0.258 ± 0.076 | −0.023 ± 0.112 | +0.017 ± 0.012 |

**Decision.** The access manipulation worked and all three direct solo comparisons improved. The registered reversal gate failed in all three lineages. Each established the old preference, sampled both colors more often and met the stationary solo and survival safeguards. Each missed absolute late local preference, absolute reversal solo score, the stationary-adjusted transfer gain and reversal exposure survival. Access changed both information and calories. Deaths contributed zero at readout. These data do not identify a preference that was acquired and subsequently forgotten. [Full estimates and gates](../results/diagnostics/E132-comparison.json)

Two research chats compared an observable contact gate on Hebbian updates, an explicit context memory and direct selection on a reversal objective. Both favor testing the contact gate first. E129 demonstrated what a scheduled learning pause could preserve after acquisition. Give the new gate, the native updater and a frequency-matched sparse updater identical histories, then probe both immediately and after a gap. Only a later prospective intervention can establish whether this improves independent choices. If it does, compare evolved descendants with their parents with the gate active from birth. A hand-coded belief in this two-color world could encode the answer, and a change in harvest after selection alone would leave the cause unresolved.

### E133 · Preregistration · 22 November 2026

Test whether locally gating the existing Hebbian rule improves acquisition, retention and carried choice after the same sparse feedback. Compare ordinary updates, one update at the locally observed outcome and one equally sparse update before that outcome. Restore ordinary updates only during the empty gap in a clone of the contact-trained state to test the retention mechanism. Positive immediate reversal in the ordinary and gated arms is required before crediting preservation. The last return processes the last contact before the delay. The [protocol](E133_PROTOCOL.md) freezes observations, body, assignments, dose, survival safeguards, minimum effects and failure decisions. No E133 scientific measurements have been made.

#### E133 prospective control correction · 22 November 2026

Replace the originally proposed fixed outward-tick control update with one independently drawn uniform offset (0–3) per treatment block, shared across both rule replays and histories. Draw these offsets on a seventh, independent sparse-timing stream and retain them with every family. Report how often they coincide with real contact feedback, which they can do by chance. The control still spends one update per block and none in the empty interval. Effects, gates, worlds, stages and budget are unchanged. The only check before this correction was the synthetic red fixture failing to import the unimplemented module. No executable treatment or scientific family was measured.

### E133 result and decision · 22 November 2026

**The contact gate failed adoption in all three lineages.** The independent audit reconstructed identical sensory input, executed actions and clipped energy feedback across policies within each history, and checked 192 final and four development families with 223 file hashes. The original saved controllers and assigned rules were kept fixed. Every creature survived the matched exposure. [Independent audit](../results/diagnostics/E133-independent-audit.json) · [Preregistration](E133_PROTOCOL.md)

| Lineage | Native immediate S | Contact immediate S | Contact change over gap minus native change | Delayed contact minus reopened S | Delayed contact solo Y | Solo gain vs native | Solo gain vs equal dose |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| [A0](../runs/20261122-212004_E133-A0) | −0.467 ± 0.008 | −0.471 ± 0.008 | −0.209 ± 0.013 | −0.113 ± 0.013 | −0.766 ± 0.053 | −0.484 ± 0.080 | +0.063 ± 0.063 |
| [A1](../runs/20261122-212134_E133-A1) | −0.405 ± 0.012 | −0.454 ± 0.009 | −0.046 ± 0.012 | −0.095 ± 0.016 | −0.719 ± 0.065 | −0.125 ± 0.085 | +0.070 ± 0.070 |
| [A2](../runs/20261122-212228_E133-A2) | −0.455 ± 0.011 | −0.450 ± 0.008 | −0.206 ± 0.017 | −0.128 ± 0.014 | −0.766 ± 0.053 | −0.516 ± 0.079 | +0.078 ± 0.056 |

S is signed adjacent-item preference and Y is food minus poison on first solo contact, with no contact and death before contact assigned zero. Each ± is one family standard error, paired for differences. Common old-food S was 0.709 ± 0.007, 0.695 ± 0.008 and 0.679 ± 0.009. Neither native nor contact-gated controllers acquired a positive new-food preference by the immediate probe. The contact gate did weaken the obsolete preference compared with the equal-dose schedule. Its delayed S gains over equal dose were 0.187 ± 0.010, 0.155 ± 0.011 and 0.184 ± 0.013. The random control's realized contact-feedback overlap was 26.0 ± 1.7%, 25.6 ± 1.7% and 23.0 ± 1.7%.

The retained choice in the gated arm was still obsolete. The same-state reopening ablation moved preference toward the new food in all three lineages and no delayed local reversal cleared the gate. The study therefore did not establish retention of a newly learned preference. Delayed solo score was lower than native in the observed means, with differences exceeding two paired SE in A0 and A2. The equal-dose solo comparison missed its registered advantage in all three. Reversal solo survival differences versus native were −24.2 ± 4.6, −25.8 ± 4.9 and −35.2 ± 5.2 percentage points. Choice probes used ordinary updating in all arms. These outcomes reflect carried state after matched histories. [Full estimates, guard components and outcome rates](../results/diagnostics/E133-comparison.json) · [Generated results](../results/RESULTS.md#contact-timed-hebbian-updating-contact-gate-v1)

**Decision.** Retire this hard contact gate setting and close these held-out worlds. For the next council decision, benchmark a short, separately preregistered evolutionary selection study on the unchanged 155-number genome. Score candidates on their own choices after matched experience, then compare frozen descendants with their parents on fresh ordinary grids. Include an equally trained stationary-objective arm to attribute a gain to selection for reversal. E122 offers a laptop timing precedent for the scale of a short study. Transfer from guided experience and autonomous recovery from birth remain distinct tests.

**Verification and cost.** The scientific source was `6c8a2f3845b691abef1cb8a9a3a3d505b5a90887`. Readiness passed 40 tests and seven independent synthetic groups. The four-family rehearsal and its audit used 21.213 and 13.920 seconds. The forecast passed at 71.357 seconds per final panel and 196.690 for the joint audit including conservative timing allowances. Final guarded wall times were 93.949, 63.816 and 80.792 seconds, with 76.019 seconds for the joint audit. Reporting verified that 69 older diagnostic JSON files kept their exact bytes. Total local charge was 415.774/3,600 seconds, leaving 5,170.382 seconds in the daily ledger. No external verification or publication occurred. [Development forecast](../results/diagnostics/E133-development-forecast.json) · [Local operational record](../results/diagnostics/E133-operations.json)
### E134 preregistration · 22 November 2026 · Select native genomes for their own choices

The owner assigned this study after E133's independent reconstruction passed and every lineage missed the architecture gate. Verified result commit `ad21926e166b1a8d3b58d845ee2e4bf79b2335de`, tag `v0.22-contact-gate-v1`, and the operator's project compute-slot release before integrating this registration. The independent audit passed with three panels and 223 checked files; the replicated E133 gate passed in zero of three. Retire E133's tested gate. Under [E134_PROTOCOL.md](E134_PROTOCOL.md), select each native E103 generation-200 genome for its developed controller's own expected signed choice after matched stationary and reversed experiences. The replay fixes intake and contributes no selection reward. Ten attempts, sixteen candidates and four fresh families per attempt are fixed. Compare each endpoint with its own parent in 128 new independent families per lineage, with a distinct gap schedule, rule-uninformative coherent feedback and fifty-turn ordinary solo probes.

Prediction is that all three descendants establish old preference, reverse after informative experience, gain at least 0.05 in signed local and solo choice over their respective parents, show the registered feedback interaction, and retain stationary skill and survival within the strict five-point lower bounds. Every protocol component must pass in every lineage. Complete failures or inconclusive components close this setting at the original thresholds. Integrity/resource failure leaves the comparison open. No checkpoint selection or tuning uses held-out worlds.

A search of existing protocol, runner and audit sources found no prior use of the eight masters or namespace `0xE134`. The registered development reconstruction checks actual key-role disjointness before final release. Final family keys stay sealed until all three endpoints are pinned. Development uses two attempts and a separate cold evaluation process on four families. Forecast the complete three-lineage training and held-out schedule before releasing final training. The fixed 128-family count remains fixed if resources fail admission. Existing all-Seed accounting and resource safeguards apply within 3,600 inclusive local seconds. All evidence, including negative or stopped work, stays local.

#### E134 prospective audit and costing clarification · 22 November 2026

Before any E134 measurements, lock the cold development evaluation order as `(8,16,0), (16,32,0), (8,32,1), (16,16,1)` for old-contact count, gap ticks and sham mapping. The first two families exercise both chamber and gap lengths. The last two measure fully warm calls. Forecast all cold costs once and charge the remaining 126 per-panel families at 1.5 times the slower warm call. The final schedule remains independently permuted and balanced. Require the audit to verify that each sham matches both dummy labels and the informative branch with the same physical payoff mapping exactly. Exercise H=2 for a perfect current-safe chooser and H=0 when choices agree across histories. Report first-food, first-poison, no-contact and descriptive conditional-on-contact accuracy with its denominator and null at zero. Better sampling can increase signed solo performance. Interpret the two delays as performance after gaps. The primary claim concerns feedback-dependent local choice and better free-solo performance after matched histories. No gate or final sample changes.
